# Test-suite audit (v0.19 baseline, requirement G)

Measured by the operator on 2026-09-06 before the v0.19 launch, read-only, on the host that runs the lanes (28 cores). This file is the baseline every G target in `docs/prds/v0.19-a-note-is-not-a-rejection.md` is measured against, and the map of what to consolidate; the run appends the § "Subsumed" table (deleted node → surviving node) as it lands G.3–G.6. The four failures in the timing run below were environmental: the operator's then-uncommitted v0.19 PRD, index row and petitions file tripped the "directory is discovered, not enumerated" guards.


Measured 2026-09-06 on this host (28 cores, load ~1), repo at 68c6692 with the v0.19 PRD,
index row and petitions file staged by the operator. Read-only audit; scratch in /tmp only.

## 2. Timing (`--durations=80`, full fast tier)

Result: **981 s (16:21)**, 6,221 passed, 4 failed, 5 skipped. The 4 failures are environmental
(staged-but-uncommitted `v0.19` PRD/report from the live run trip the "directory is discovered not
enumerated" guards in `tests/test_docs_prd_index.py`, `test_report_claims.py`, `tests/test_sweep_sha_guard.py`); not a
suite defect, but a reminder that record guards depend on working-tree state.

Where the time goes:

| bucket | evidence | est. share |
|---|---|---|
| slowest 80 tests | sum 599 s of 981 s; all are real-engine + stub-pi or real sleeps | 61 % |
| single worst test | `test_interrupt_not_substantive_outcome_task004::test_3_mutation_...` **80.3 s** -- the mutated path falls into the contract ladder and hits an unstubbed `_wait_out_backoff` (loop.py:1389/3257) | 8 % |
| next | `test_reflect_infra_retry::test_reflect_survives_the_outage...` 35 s; `tests/test_refunds_survive_resume.py` 22 s; `tests/test_durations.py` 25 s/2 (STUB_SLEEP=3); `tests/test_cli_logs_follow_liveness.py` 37 s/4; `test_e2e.py` 46 s/6 in the top 80 | |
| `ralphctl` subprocess CLI tests | group of 7 cli modules: 320 tests in 17.3 s (~55 ms each, interpreter start + imports) -> 64 modules ~150-200 s | ~18 % |
| nested `pytest --collect-only` per node id | `test_conformance_ledger.py`: 153 tests, 16.4 s | 2 % |
| bash `scripts/host_like_*.sh` runs | `test_host_like_*`: 134 tests, 10.7 s | 1 % |
| docs/spec derivation checks | `test_docs_consistency/semantics/prd_index/spec_*/sweep_sha`: 224 tests, 11.1 s | 1 % |
| in-process engine + prose pins | e.g. 7 lint/gate modules: 368 tests in 0.9 s; 9 roadmap modules: 106 tests in 0.45 s; report_claims 1,100 in 2.0 s | ~10 % for all ~4,000 |

Per-module slow time in the top 80: `test_interrupt_not_substantive_outcome_task004.py` 80 s,
`tests/test_e2e.py` 46 s, `tests/test_cli_logs_follow_liveness.py` 37 s, `tests/test_reflect_infra_retry.py` 35 s, `tests/test_cli_ui.py`
26 s, `tests/test_durations.py` 25 s, `tests/test_refunds_survive_resume.py` 24 s, `tests/test_resume_by_state.py` 22 s,
`tests/test_iteration_timeout_contained.py` 21 s, `tests/test_inband_error_retry.py` 19 s, `tests/test_config_overlay.py` 17 s,
`test_steer_tool_spool_two_uid_task030.py` 16 s, `test_crash_consistency.py` 16 s,
`tests/test_status_health_infra_wait.py` 14 s, `test_infra_outage_budget.py` 14 s.

**Key finding:** the pinned-prose/per-task tests are a *count and maintenance* problem, not a
wall-clock problem (4,000 in-process tests run in roughly 100 s). Wall clock is ~75 % real engines,
real sleeps and subprocess spawns, and is almost perfectly parallelisable.

## 3. Static analysis

- **Real-engine tests are spread thin.** 44 modules / ~175 tests each spawn `ralphd-engine` +
  `tests/stub-pi` (1,301-line stub with ~45 `STUB_*` knobs). `STUB_SLEEP` is set to 1-5 s in 42
  call sites (15 use 0). `test_e2e.py` carried its own `EngineProc` that duplicated
  `conftest.LiveRun` (folded into `LiveRun`, with both layouts behind `registry=`, by task 021,
  requirement G.1; the ~47 modules that imported it now import `tests.conftest.LiveRun`). Loop paths covered by a real engine today: happy path, review rejection ->
  new approach, stagnation, budget/grace review, steering (4 variants), abort, huge line, vigilant
  verify fail/transient/three strikes, criteria tamper, lock, logs follow, peer gates (task029/031/
  032), ladder exhaustion (task033), cure pass (task023), budget top-up. **Not covered by any
  real-engine test:** petition + adjudication (24 in-process modules, 0 e2e), last-approach pause
  + resume (3 in-process modules), deadline/pause credit, contract re-ask ladder end-to-end.
- **Harness duplication.** 87 modules define `_supervisor()`; 98 modules build `IterationResult`
  by hand; 14 assign `sup._run_iteration_once = fake`, 7 `sup.run_iteration = fake`, 6 stub
  `_wait_out_backoff`. The rest inherit real sleeps (hence the 80 s and 35 s tests).
- **Per-task pins overlap by feature.** Last-approach pause: `test_last_approach_pause_task011.py` (9), `test_last_approach_pause_persisted_task036.py`
  (9), `resume_..._task024` (2). Cure pass: `test_cure_pass_task009.py` (6), `test_cure_pass_not_on_tamper_task039.py` (6),
  `test_bounded_cure_pass_task010.py` (5), `test_cure_pass_id_collision_task006.py` (4), `test_cure_pass_e2e_task023.py` (1). Contract ladder: 7 modules.
  Steer: 14 modules. Eight modules re-pin the same transition-table edges
  (`test_the_edge_is_declared_in_both_variants`, 29 `transitions.is_legal/successors` call sites).
- **Prose pins inside engine modules.** Of the 100 guard modules that *do* import ralphd, 320 of
  1,591 test functions read a `.md` in the body (e.g. `tests/test_docs_semantics.py` 23, `tests/test_suggestions_patch.py`
  16, `test_seventh_lint_task013.py` 14, `tests/test_cli_suggestions_verb.py` 12, `tests/test_hub_artifacts.py` 12).
  `docs/cli.md` is read by 34 modules, `SPEC.md` by 29, `architecture.md` by 24, `roadmap.md` by 11.
- **Parametrised explosions:** `test_report_claims.py` (217 reports x 5), `test_conformance_ledger.py`
  (79 citations + 68 node ids, one `pytest` subprocess each), `tests/test_host_like_pytest_script.py`
  (quantifier x template paraphrase grid, ~100 regex cases). No `parametrize` list >8 literal items
  elsewhere.
- **pytest-xdist:** not installed (`pytest 9.1.1`, `pytest-asyncio 1.4.0`); `pip download
  pytest-xdist` succeeds (3.8.0), so it is installable. Shared-state audit for `-n auto`:
  ports are always `free_port()` (bind-0, TOCTOU race only); HOME is isolated per test by the
  autouse fixture; all writes go to `tmp_path`; no module writes into the repo tree; 3 direct
  `os.environ[...] =` mutations (`test_iteration_env_strip:118`, `test_e2e:1066`,
  `test_sibling_cleanup_job_safe:154` [docker tier]); 1 `monkeypatch.chdir` (`test_image_dockerfile:123`);
  3 cwd-relative `Path("docs/...")` uses; module-scoped fixtures only in docker-tier modules;
  2 `AMBIENT_HOME`-marked modules read the real HOME; `tests/test_skills_discoverability_survives_suite.py`
  runs a nested pytest subset (8 s). Nothing here blocks xdist; the two `os.environ` writes in the
  fast tier should become `monkeypatch.setenv`.

## 4. Proposal

### (a) Consolidate into black-box flow tests
Replace the 44 real-engine modules (~175 tests, ~600 s) with **one module per loop path, ~12 flows,
each asserting many facts along one run** (status.json, `--json status`, events.jsonl, logs
merge/tail/follow, `runs`, `artifacts`, hub JSON). Target ~15-20 s each serial with `STUB_SLEEP=0`
and backoffs set to 0 via job.yaml:

1. happy path + exit modes + durations + cost breakdown + artifacts (absorbs `tests/test_durations.py`,
   `tests/test_cli_cost_breakdown.py`, `tests/test_cli_iteration_detail.py`, `tests/test_cli_artifacts.py`, `test_cli_runs_*`, `test_cli_logs*` x11)
2. validation rejection -> retry -> three strikes -> review; criteria tamper refusal (absorbs vigilant `tests/test_e2e.py` cases, `test_criteria_tamper_refusal_task024.py`)
3. review rejection -> replan -> ladder exhaustion -> last-approach pause -> resume with/without ruling (absorbs `test_ladder_exhaustion_e2e_task033.py`, 3 last-approach modules, `tests/test_resume_by_state.py`)
4. cure pass: curable-only -> cure tasks -> re-review; bounded; not on tamper; id collision (absorbs 5 cure-pass modules)
5. petition -> adjudication -> refuted/confirmed -> history persisted (new e2e; today 0 real-engine coverage)
6. peer gates: plan-review objections, verdict-gate reject/malformed/peek (absorbs task029/031/032, `test_peer_*`)
7. steer: mid-review, mid-verify, redelivery, uncharged, steer tool spool, priority routing (absorbs 14 steer modules' engine halves)
8. infra: instant fail, hang, in-band error, outage budget, reflect retry -- **with `_wait_out_backoff` stubbed / backoff config 0** (absorbs `test_*infra*`, `tests/test_inband_error_retry.py`, `tests/test_reflect_infra_retry.py`: ~90 s today)
9. crash/resume: SIGKILL at boundary, mid-review, mid-reflect; refunds survive resume; lock semantics (absorbs `test_crash_consistency.py`, `tests/test_refunds_survive_resume.py`, `test_vigilant_crash_resume.py`)
10. budget/deadline: exhaustion, grace review once, top-up mid-flight, deadline beats verdict, pause-deadline credit
11. control plane: abort, API token, health/infra-wait JSON, secret redaction, huge/bad NDJSON line
12. hub over a live engine: run detail, steering POST, task counts, delete (absorbs `tests/test_cli_ui.py`, `tests/test_hub_steering.py`)

Delete/merge: the 65 per-task modules fold into their feature module (keep the *assertion*, drop
the module); the 87 `_supervisor` copies become two conftest fixtures (`supervisor(tmp_path, **cfg)`,
`scripted_attempts(sup, [...])` that also stubs every backoff); the 8 transition-edge re-pins
become one `test_transitions.py`; `test_e2e.EngineProc` is deleted in favour of `LiveRun`.

CLI: 64 modules / 159 `ralphctl` spawns -> call `cli.main.main()` in-process with argv + capsys
except where the process boundary is the subject (`tests/test_cli_logs_signal.py`, `_pty_termios`,
`_follow_liveness`, `tests/test_ralphctl_resolution.py`, `tests/test_engine_child_import_path.py`). Saves ~150 s.

### (b) Records lint: one fast module family, kept
Move to `tests/records/` (so `final_sha_guards.sh` selects a directory, not a grep) and keep:
`test_report_claims.py` (collapse to 5 tests that iterate all 217 reports and list every problem),
`tests/test_sweep_sha_guard.py`, `test_conformance_ledger.py` (replace 68 `pytest` spawns with one in-process
`pytest --collect-only` over all node ids: 16 s -> <1 s -- DONE at task 033, `tests/records/test_conformance_ledger.py`), `tests/test_docs_consistency.py`,
`tests/test_docs_semantics.py`, `tests/test_spec_cli_surface.py`, `tests/test_spec_state_surface.py`, `tests/test_docs_prd_index.py`,
`tests/test_roadmap_terminal_claims.py`, `tests/test_issue_traceability.py`, `tests/test_commit_closing_keyword_scope.py`,
`tests/test_final_sha_guards_discovery.py`, the transition table/diagram docs checks. Fold the 8
`test_roadmap_v0N.py` into one parametrised `test_roadmap_blocks.py` (106 -> ~15 tests) -- DONE at
task 034, `tests/records/test_roadmap.py`: the 96 collected nodes across the 8 sources (not 106; that
estimate double-counted) fold 1:1 into 96 nodes in the merged module -- every checker and its own
mutation twin survives named, none collapsed or parametrised away, since the task's own criteria ask
for the fold method D.7 used (per-source rename on collision), not a node-count reduction. Keep exactly
one "the check is substantive" mutation per *checker* (47 exist today across these modules), never
per fact. Prose pins that live inside engine modules (320 test bodies) move here or are deleted when
they only restate a sentence. Budget: <=300 tests, <15 s, zero subprocesses except one `git ls-files`.

### (c) Parallelise
Yes. Add `pytest-xdist` to the dev extras and run the fast tier with `-n 8 --dist loadfile`.
Nothing structural blocks it (section 3). Fixes needed first: the two `os.environ[...] =` writes ->
`monkeypatch.setenv`; `free_port()` retry-on-EADDRINUSE in `LiveRun`; `AMBIENT_HOME` tests and
`tests/test_skills_discoverability_survives_suite.py` marked `@pytest.mark.xdist_group` or run serial. Expected on this host:
the 600 s of engine/sleep time is I/O-bound and independent -> ~80-100 s at `-n 8`; the in-process
remainder ~50 s. Fast tier ~3 min *before* any consolidation; ~1.5-2 min after.

### (d) Tiers
| lane | contents | serial est. | `-n 8` est. |
|---|---|---|---|
| **smoke** (per-task verify hat) | in-process engine flows (LoopSupervisor + scripted attempts, backoffs stubbed), CLI in-process, records lint, hub in-process | ~90-120 s | ~30 s |
| **fast** (final sha / PR) | smoke + the 12 stub-pi flows + process-boundary CLI tests | ~5-6 min | ~2 min |
| **nightly** | docker tier (~20 min), browser (~8 min), the two dev-layout modes (today 40 min each; scale with the fast tier -> ~15 min each) | ~1.5 h | ~45 min |

The three lanes are markers as of task 004 (requirement G.8): `smoke` and `fast` are registered in
`pyproject.toml` and derived per item by `pytest_collection_modifyitems` in `tests/conftest.py` from
exactly this table's contents column (nightly is the pre-existing `docker`/`browser` pair plus the
dev-layout module). `docs/testing.md` carries the recipes and the measured budget per lane, and
`tests/test_tier_budgets.py` fails when a lane outgrows the budget declared there.

### (e) Risks: what the merged flows must still catch
- Ordering/precedence bugs the pins were written for: incurable-over-curable, tamper never cures,
  cure pass once per approach, last-approach pauses instead of failing, petition adjudication
  persisted before notify, refunds not double-applied on resume. Each flow must assert *sequence*
  (events.jsonl order), not only terminal state.
- Mutation discipline: keep one paired mutation per *checker/discriminator* (`_iteration_cut_short_by_engine`,
  lint regexes, record parsers) with backoffs stubbed; drop per-fact mutations.
- Flaky timing: flows must poll status/events with deadlines, never `STUB_SLEEP>0` for
  "measurable duration" -- assert `durationSeconds >= 0` and monotonicity instead (`tests/test_durations.py` 25 s -> <2 s).
- Loss of "diff-shaped" failure messages: a merged flow that fails must print which step; use
  `pytest.fail(f"step {name}: ...")` helpers or `subtests`.
- Record guards keyed to working-tree state (today's 4 failures): run them on committed state or
  make discovery use `git ls-files`.

### (f) Ordered plan (PRD-ready requirements with measurable criteria)
1. **Shared harness.** conftest fixtures `supervisor`/`scripted_attempts(sup, [...])` (backoffs and sleeps
   stubbed by default); delete all 87 module-local `_supervisor` and `test_e2e.EngineProc`.
   *Criterion:* `grep -c 'def _supervisor(' tests/` == 0; no fast-tier test >10 s in `--durations`.
2. **Kill real sleeps.** Stub `_wait_out_backoff` in every mutation test; `STUB_SLEEP=0` everywhere;
   backoff config 0 in flow job.yaml. *Criterion:* sum of slowest 80 < 120 s (today 599 s).
3. **xdist.** Add `pytest-xdist`, fix the 2 env writes and the port race, document `-n 8
   --dist loadfile` in `scripts/host_like_pytest.sh` and the verify hat. *Criterion:* fast tier
   green at `-n 8` three consecutive runs; wall clock < 4 min.
4. **Records lint module family** (section b) in `tests/records/`; `final_sha_guards.sh` selects the
   directory. *Criterion:* records lane <= 300 tests, < 15 s, `test_report_claims.py` <= 10 tests,
   `test_conformance_ledger.py` spawns 0 subprocesses per node id, roadmap modules == 1.
5. **Twelve stub-pi flows** (section a) replacing the 44 real-engine modules; petition/adjudication
   and last-approach pause gain real-engine coverage. *Criterion:* modules using `live(`/`engine_factory`
   <= 14; every flow asserts an events.jsonl sequence; each flow < 20 s serial.
6. **Fold per-task pins.** Every `tests/test_*_taskNNN.py` either merges into its feature module or
   is deleted; no surviving module opens with "Task NNN". *Criterion:* `ls tests/test_*_task*.py`
   empty; feature modules for last-approach, cure pass, contract ladder, steer, petitions each == 1.
7. **CLI in-process.** Convert `ralphctl` subprocess call sites to `main()` + capsys except the
   process-boundary modules. *Criterion (behavioural, never a grep count -- operator ruling on task
   001, steer 009 section 4):* `tests/records/test_ralphctl_process_boundary.py` derives, from each
   module's own AST (an unoverridden `LiveRun.ralphctl()` call site, or a spawn argv naming the
   resolved `RALPHCTL` path, found in that module's own source only -- never chased through a
   fixture import into another module's body, the shape that produces false positives), the set of
   `tests/test_cli*.py` modules that spawn a real process. That set is exactly 6, <= the PRD's cap
   of 8: `test_cli_logs_pty_termios.py`, `test_cli_logs_signal.py`, `test_cli_watch_resumed_run.py`,
   `test_cli_logs_follow_liveness.py`, `test_cli_logs_resumed_run.py`, `test_cli_ui.py` -- each
   justified in § "Requirement 7's process-boundary allowlists" below. The same module's second
   node pins the tree-wide set (every `tests/test_*.py` module) at exactly 32: those 6 plus 26
   modules elsewhere in the tree whose own subject is a real `ralphctl`-subprocess contract that
   this conversion never targeted, each named with its reason in the same section. cli family < 30 s.
8. **Tiering + budget guard.** Markers `smoke`/`fast`; a test in `tests/records/` fails if the fast
   tier exceeds N tests or the recorded `--durations` budget is blown. *Criterion:* smoke < 2 min
   serial; fast tier < 5 min serial / < 2 min `-n 8`; total collected <= 3,000 tests (today 6,230);
   modules <= 180 (today 383); the two dev-layout modes < 15 min each.

Net effect: 6,230 -> ~3,000 tests, 383 -> ~180 modules, 16.3 min -> ~5 min serial / ~2 min
parallel, with petition/adjudication and last-approach pause gaining the end-to-end coverage they
lack today.

### Requirement 7's process-boundary allowlists (task 005)

Both allowlists below are pinned live by `tests/records/test_ralphctl_process_boundary.py`; a
locally mutated copy of either one fails that module's corresponding node. The rule that decides
membership is behavioural (an AST walk over each module's OWN source; see the module's own
docstring), never a `grep -c 'RALPHCTL'` count.

**CLI scope -- `tests/test_cli*.py`, 6 of 63 modules (<= the PRD's cap of 8):**

| module | why it needs a real process |
|---|---|
| `test_cli_logs_pty_termios.py` | a real pty plus termios state carried across the child process |
| `test_cli_logs_signal.py` | real `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM` delivery to a real child and `rc == 128 + signal.SIGTERM` |
| `test_cli_watch_resumed_run.py` | a `--json watch` stream drained concurrently with a live engine |
| `test_cli_logs_follow_liveness.py` | a follow whose stdin is an open-but-unwritten `subprocess.PIPE` |
| `test_cli_logs_resumed_run.py` | its `Follower` class observes a live streaming follow across a real engine resume -- task 033's own audit subject, not reproducible via a blocking in-process capsys capture |
| `test_cli_ui.py` | hosts the `UiServer` class that ~15 modules outside CLI scope construct directly for real-subprocess/playwright coverage; its DELETE-route dependency shells out to real `docker`, which only resolves its env correctly in a genuine child process (task 004's record) |

**Tree-wide -- every `tests/test_*.py` module, 32 of ~317 (the 6 above plus 26 more):**

| module | why it is a real process boundary |
|---|---|
| `test_debt_ledger.py` | reads `ralphctl docs`'s real stdout so the debt ledger is checked through the actual host-side CLI contract, not the in-process object |
| `test_docker_sibling_e2e.py` | `@pytest.mark.docker` container-level e2e; every command is a real subprocess by design, and the module skips cleanly when docker is unreachable |
| `test_docs_semantics.py` | drives `ralphctl ... watch` as a real piped subprocess to check the semantic doc pass's claims against genuine CLI behaviour |
| `test_durations.py` | reads the human-readable duration lines off the real `status`/`logs` stdout a human actually sees |
| `test_engine_child_import_path.py` | its whole subject is whether a real grandchild interpreter, launched with an overridden `HOME`, can still import `ralphd` -- unrepresentable without an actual subprocess |
| `test_hub_artifacts.py` | seeds a scratch `RALPHD_REGISTRY` and reads it back through a real `ralphctl` child so the hub dialog's server-side JSON is checked against the real CLI's own answer |
| `test_hub_cost_dialog.py` | same `RALPHD_REGISTRY`-scoped real-child pattern, for the cost-breakdown dialog |
| `test_hub_delete.py` | the delete endpoint under test shells out to real `docker` itself; its own `ralphctl rm --force` comparison call must be a real sibling process for the two deletion paths to be comparable |
| `test_hub_fault_dialog.py` | same `RALPHD_REGISTRY`-scoped real-child pattern, for the fault dialog |
| `test_hub_iteration_dialog.py` | same `RALPHD_REGISTRY`-scoped real-child pattern, for the iteration-detail dialog |
| `test_hub_run_documents.py` | same `RALPHD_REGISTRY`-scoped real-child pattern, for the state-document dialogs |
| `test_image_build.py` | what a genuinely separate `ralphctl start` process resolves as "the image" under a stub docker binary selected by real subprocess env vars (`RALPHD_DOCKER`, `STUB_DOCKER_IMAGES`) -- there is no in-process image-resolution path to substitute |
| `test_image_packaged_inputs.py` | same real-subprocess image-resolution pattern, for the packaged (`pipx install`) case |
| `test_image_recorded.py` | same real-subprocess image-resolution pattern, for the resume-reproduces-the-image case |
| `test_no_transcript_message.py` | reproduces the empty-`iterations/` rendering as the real CLI prints it to a real child's stdout |
| `test_operator_ruling.py` | drives `ralphctl amend` as a real subprocess including piped stdin, the actual operator-facing entry point |
| `test_ralphctl_resolution.py` | its whole subject is whether `RALPHCTL`/`RALPHD_ENGINE` resolve to an executable file at all -- `os.access(..., os.X_OK)` on a real path IS the test |
| `test_reflect_tombstone_cleared.py` | drives `ralphctl` across a real fail-then-resume-then-succeed cycle to see the tombstone file disappear from the real run dir a real process wrote to |
| `test_rm_force_finishing_docs.py` | exercises `ralphctl rm --force` on a run mid-`finishing` window; the real return code and stderr text are the documented contract under test |
| `test_sibling_cleanup_job_safe.py` | a real `ralphctl stop` subprocess proves the job container survives sibling-only cleanup -- the guarantee is about what a real process does, not a text assertion |
| `test_spec_cli_surface.py` | drives every SPEC.md-documented verb through a real child to prove the table is the real surface, not wherever the in-process shortcut happens to route |
| `test_status_health_infra_wait.py` | reads `--json status` off a real child so the degraded-state contract is checked as an external reader would see it |
| `test_status_max_approaches.py` | same real-child `--json status` pattern, for the `maxApproaches` field |
| `test_steering_consumed_on_success.py` | drives `ralphctl steer --list` as a real subprocess to read consumption state the way an operator's shell would |
| `test_tasks_stale_cli.py` | drives `ralphctl tasks`/`--json tasks` as a real subprocess specifically because its subject is the *host-side* reader falling back to disk when the engine is not the one answering |
| `test_tier_cost_breakdown.py` | same real-child `--json status` pattern, for the per-tier spend/iteration-count breakdown |

None of the 26 non-CLI members are in `tests/test_cli*.py`'s scope (the family requirement 7
converts); each predates or sits outside that conversion and was never a candidate for it.

### Real-engine spawn count after tasks 020/021 (requirements C.9/C.10)

The audit's own target for the twelve flows is section 4(f) item 5: *"modules
using `live(`/`engine_factory` <= 14"*. Measured on this tree, at the commit
that folded the second half:

```
grep -lE '(^|[^_a-zA-Z])live\(|engine_factory\(' tests/*.py | grep -v conftest.py | wc -l
```

**55** modules under `tests/` still name the shared real-engine spawn helper
(the twelve `tests/flows/` modules are not counted by that command and are the
consolidation's own destination, not part of its residue). That is over the
target of 14, so this section takes the disclosure route the C.10 criterion
allows and NAMES every module still counted -- the number without the names
would be unauditable, and none of these were in tasks 020/021's remit (which
was the audit's own list of real-engine *loop-path* modules the flows now
cover, 10 modules / 27 nodes, all deleted):

Reading the list: the great majority are CLI, cost/usage, hub and record
modules that spawn an engine only to have a live run to address (`test_cli_*`
21 of the 55, `test_cost_*`/`test_pricing_map.py`/`test_usage_accounting.py` 4)
-- requirement F's subject, not C's -- plus `tests/test_e2e.py` itself and the
infra/resume/reflect family whose remaining nodes assert engine behaviour no
flow claims. Recompute the number with the command above rather than quoting
this paragraph: it moves with every F-series conversion.

## Subsumed

Filled in by the v0.19 run as requirement G lands: one row per deleted test node, naming the
surviving flow/records test that covers the behaviour it pinned. A deleted node with no row here
is a blocking finding under the PRD's rubric.

Derivation for task 006 (requirement G.9): diff every `-def test_`/`+def test_` line out of
`git diff 4c003a5 HEAD -- tests/` and keep the removed names that never reappear as an added
name anywhere in the same diff (a same-name signature change, e.g. gaining a fixture argument,
is not a removal). At this table's construction that git-derived list is exactly two names:
`test_a_distinct_blocker_signature_keeps_the_remaining_approaches` (was in
`tests/test_stagnation_signature.py`) and `test_three_ordinary_work_noop_iterations_still_fail_the_approach`
(was in `tests/test_stagnation_excludes_nonwork_faults.py`) -- both rows below. `tests/test_subsumed_table.py`
resolves each row's surviving node against the collected test set on every run, so a later
deletion the table forgets to record is caught mechanically, not just at construction time.

Derivation for task 020 (requirement C.9): the same `-def test_` diff, run over that task's own
deletion of five real-engine modules (`git show <sha>^:<path> | grep '^def test'` for each), gives
exactly eleven deleted nodes -- four in `test_e2e_peer_gates_task029.py`, one in
`test_e2e_verdict_gate_second_approach_task031.py`, two in
`test_e2e_composed_gates_task032.py`, one in `test_cure_pass_e2e_task023.py`, three in
`test_infra_fault_retry.py` -- and each has its OWN row below (a module-level row covering
several nodes at once is not what this table claims). Two of those eleven had no surviving cover
when the modules were deleted; rather than disclose them as narrowings, task 020 added the missing
cover to the flows themselves -- `tests/flows/test_flow_peer_gates.py`'s two composed nodes
(vigilant + `verdict_gate`, and both peer gates in one run) and
`tests/flows/test_flow_infra_ladder.py`'s startup-window timing node -- so every row below names a
surviving node that asserts the deleted node's own claim.

Derivation for task 021 (requirement C.10): the same `-def test_` diff, run over this task's own
deletion of `test_crash_consistency.py` and `test_vigilant_crash_resume.py`, gives exactly three
deleted nodes -- two in the former, one in the latter. Unlike task 020's five modules, all three
nodes were moved into `tests/flows/test_flow_crash_resume_refunds.py` essentially VERBATIM (same
assertions, same hold-then-SIGKILL-then-resume shape, only the module and its two shared helpers
changed) rather than absorbed into a differently-shaped flow, so each row below names the exact
same-named function in its new home.

diff, run over this task's own deletion of the five remaining real-engine

Derivation for task 027 (requirement D.6): eight modules whose whole subject is the declared run-state transition table itself, or one specific edge in it -- `test_transition_table.py` (008/#72), `test_transition_table_docs.py` (016), `test_transition_diagram_docs.py` (005), `test_transition_executor_cutover.py` (014), `test_rejected_incurable_edge.py` (task 006, B, #111), `test_steer_hat_table_entry.py` (task 009 v0.14, G, #98), `test_doomed_plan_fault.py` (task 007 v0.14, D, #99), `test_plan_review_resume_issue161.py` (task 046, #161) -- are folded, whole, into `tests/test_transitions.py`: 97 nodes in, 97 nodes out, every one verbatim except three renamed to resolve a genuine name collision between `test_transition_table_docs.py` and `test_transition_diagram_docs.py` (both independently named `test_markers_present_exactly_once`/`test_doc_block_matches_the_generator_right_now`/`test_render_is_idempotent`; the diagram-docs section's three survive suffixed `_in_the_diagram`/`test_diagram_block_matches_the_generator_right_now`/`test_diagram_render_is_idempotent`). Each row below names one deleted node; the audit's own "eight modules re-pin the same transition-table edges" figure (§3) is the two already-foundational table/doc/cutover modules plus the six per-edge modules the fold brings in whole -- three of the genre's earlier instances (`test_last_approach_pause_task011.py`, `test_stagnation_last_approach_pause_task012.py`, `test_cure_pass_task009.py`) were already folded into `tests/test_last_approach.py`/`tests/test_cure_pass.py` by tasks 022/023 and are not repeated here.

Derivation for task 026 (requirement D.5): the seven `test_*_task*.py` modules whose subject is petition filing/discovery, mid-run adjudication, or an adjudication's downstream effect on `tasks.json`/the run record (`test_adjudicated_petitions_history_task014.py`, `test_petitioned_hold_not_starve_task014.py`, `test_petition_confirmed_closes_task_task015.py`, `test_refuted_petitions_rendered_task002.py`, `test_refuted_adjudications_persisted_task016.py`, `test_petitions_scoped_to_wave_task018.py`, `test_petition_writeback_task019.py`) are folded, whole, into `tests/test_petitions.py` -- 73 nodes in, 73 nodes out, every one verbatim except two renamed to resolve a genuine name collision between the two REFUTED-rendering sections (both originally named the same way, pinned once per section with a different scenario; each gained a suffix naming its own scenario -- `..._with_witness_log` and `..._confirmed_store_only` below). Each row below names one deleted node.

Derivation for task 028 (requirement D.7, cluster 2): five modules whose subject is the peer verdict gate and the review-verdict label beside it -- `test_verdict_gate_findings_authorship_task019.py` (task 019, D.4, #125), `test_verdict_gate_peek_task020.py` (task 020, D.5, #125 A), `test_peer_gate_flags_task017.py` (task 017, D.3, #117 side finding), `test_plan_review_deterministic_undecidable_task016.py` (task 016, D.2, #117 half B) and `test_verified_with_residuals_task012.py` (task 012, D.3) -- are folded, whole, into a new `tests/test_verdict_gate.py`: 38 nodes in, 38 nodes out, every test body verbatim; only module-private helper names were renamed to resolve cross-module collisions between the two different scripted-agent harnesses each module reused (`test_peer_verdict_gate.py`'s `_supervisor`/`_ok`/`_plan`/`_run_job`/`_worker_completes`/`_review_verifies` vs `test_peer_plan_review_gate.py`'s own same-named-but-different fixtures, aliased `_pr_*` in the merged module; task 012's own local `_finding`/`_supervisor` renamed `_residual_finding`/`_residuals_supervisor` to avoid colliding with task 019's own `_finding` and the imported `_supervisor`) -- no test function itself was renamed.

Derivation for task 028 (requirement D.7, the sweep): the per-task pin modules whose subject is the contract/ingestion fault-event surface -- `contract.violation`, `contract.agent_fault`, the blocker signature an empty-plan ingestion writes, the criteria-tamper refusal and the per-iteration fault cause -- `test_contract_violation_task012.py`, `test_contract_violation_and_agent_fault_events_task011.py`, `test_contract_exhaustion_never_solely_ends_the_run_task009.py`, `test_ingestion_blocker_signature_task012.py`, `test_criteria_tamper_refusal_task024.py`, `test_deck_phase3g_regression_task010.py`, `test_iteration_fault_cause_task013.py` -- are folded, whole, into a new `tests/test_contract_events.py`: 28 nodes in, 28 nodes out, every test body and docstring verbatim. Derivation for task 028 (requirement D.7, the sweep): the per-task pin modules whose subject is the worker hat and the task-status vocabulary it drives -- verdict dispatch and re-entry, `plan_complete`, the retry prompt, absorption and stagnation witnesses, `failed`/`skipped` and dependent doom, `settled` vs `done`, the scheduler and the engine-owned terminal record -- `test_failed_task_vocabulary.py`, `test_stagnation_progress_witnesses_task010.py`, `test_task_absorption_handoff_task011.py`, `test_worker_dispatch_reentry_task008.py`, `test_worker_plan_complete_task015.py`, `test_worker_retry_rejection_prompt_task015.py`, `test_interrupt_not_substantive_outcome_task004.py`, `test_failed_to_skipped_dependent_doom_task011.py`, `test_failed_to_skipped_status_task009.py`, `test_settled_vs_done_task013.py`, `test_scheduler_select_next_task.py`, `test_engine_owns_terminal_record_task008.py` -- are folded, whole, into a new `tests/test_worker_hat.py`: 113 nodes in, 113 nodes out, every test body and docstring verbatim. Only module-private helper, constant and class names that collided across the merged sources were renamed (a per-source prefix; that module's own docstring lists every `old -> new` pair, and an intra-cluster `from test_<pin> import ...` became a direct reference to the same object now defined in the merged module) -- no test function was renamed. Each row below names the surviving node.

Derivation for task 028 (requirement D.7, the sweep): the per-task pin modules whose subject is the shipped prompt/skill surface -- what worker.md must say about a terminally failed task, the no-`tasks.json`-write rule across every prompt, reflect's improvement scope, the injected telegram-notify skill and stale wiring docstrings -- `test_prompt_failed_task_resolution.py`, `test_prompt_no_tasks_json_write_instruction.py`, `test_reflect_prompt_scope_task026.py`, `test_skills_notify_preference_injection_task003.py`, `test_stale_wiring_docstrings_task003.py` -- are folded, whole, into a new `tests/test_prompt_surface.py`: 25 nodes in, 25 nodes out, every test body and docstring verbatim. Only module-private helper, constant and class names that collided across the merged sources were renamed (a per-source prefix; that module's own docstring lists every `old -> new` pair, and an intra-cluster `from test_<pin> import ...` became a direct reference to the same object now defined in the merged module) -- no test function was renamed. Derivation for task 028 (requirement D.7, the sweep): the per-task pin modules whose subject is the `ralphctl` feature surface -- the budget/resume flags, `tasks --approach`, the in-flight budget setters, contract-violation surfacing in `status`/`iteration N` and the TASKS column on `runs` -- `test_cli_budget_resume_94_flags_task004.py`, `test_cli_tasks_approach.py`, `test_inflight_budget_setters_task094.py`, `test_cli_contract_violation_surfacing_task012.py`, `test_cli_runs_tasks_column.py` -- are folded, whole, into a new `tests/test_cli_features.py`: 58 nodes in, 58 nodes out, every test body and docstring verbatim. Only module-private helper, constant and class names that collided across the merged sources were renamed (a per-source prefix; that module's own docstring lists every `old -> new` pair, and an intra-cluster `from test_<pin> import ...` became a direct reference to the same object now defined in the merged module) -- no test function was renamed. Each row below names the surviving node.

Derivation for task 028 (requirement D.7, the sweep): the per-task pin modules whose subject is run secrets -- the mode-0700 run-secrets mount `ralphctl start`/`resume` bind and the MAC key the next engine episode must read back under privilege separation -- `test_cli_run_secrets_mount_task020.py`, `test_run_secrets_key_read_under_privsep_task020.py` -- are folded, whole, into a new `tests/test_run_secrets.py`: 12 nodes in, 12 nodes out, every test body and docstring verbatim. Only module-private helper, constant and class names that collided across the merged sources were renamed (a per-source prefix; that module's own docstring lists every `old -> new` pair, and an intra-cluster `from test_<pin> import ...` became a direct reference to the same object now defined in the merged module) -- no test function was renamed. Each row below names the surviving node.

Count correction for task 028's sweep (requirement D.7): the sweep folded away 39 modules holding **343** test functions -- 343 nodes in, 343 nodes out, one Subsumed row above per node (derived here, not carried: `git diff --no-renames --diff-filter=D --name-only cf20afc~1 cf20afc` gives the 39 module paths, and counting `^def test_` in each at `cf20afc~1` gives 343; the same 343 is the sum of the per-destination counts in each derivation paragraph above -- 28 + 113 + 25 + 58 + 58 + 12 + 25 + 13 + 11). Commit `cf20afc`'s own message misstates that total as "331 nodes in, 331 nodes out"; its per-destination counts are the correct ones. This line is the correction -- the commit message itself is left as it landed rather than rewritten after the push.

Derivation for task 032 (requirement E.1): `test_report_claims.py` collapsed into
`tests/records/test_report_claims.py`. Five of its ten tests were parametrised over every report
under `artifacts/reports/` (`params=REPORTS`, 231 reports at this task's own HEAD) -- 1,155 of the
old module's 1,165 collected nodes were one of these five families times 231 reports. The collapse
runs all five checks inside ONE test's own loop over the same glob-discovered report list
(`test_every_report_passes_its_claim_checks`), so every family retires as a whole rather than
node by node; a sixth deleted node pair (the two `!retired`-direction mutation tests) merged into
one test the same way, since both exercise `retired_problems` in the same direction. The other four
tests (`test_the_reports_directory_is_discovered_not_enumerated`,
`test_the_claim_checks_have_exactly_one_implementation`, `test_a_node_family_wildcard_needs_a_real_match`,
`test_path_problems_takes_at_most_one_git_ls_files_snapshot`,
`test_untracked_generated_path_fails_with_the_untracked_message`,
`test_the_same_absolute_run_dir_path_still_passes`, `test_tracked_but_missing_path_fails_with_the_other_message`,
`test_the_v05_report_still_names_its_own_module`) moved unchanged, same names, no row needed.
